<meta>This page serves as the preface to the work, establishing the foundational philosophy that human thought is a creative force governed by universal laws.</meta>

## PREFACE.

There is a golden thread that runs through every religion in the world. There is a golden thread that runs through the lives and the teachings of all the prophets, seers, sages, and saviors in the world's history, and through the lives of all men and women of truly great and lasting power. All that they have ever done or attained has been done in full accordance with law. What one has done, all may do.

This same golden thread must enter into the lives of all who today—in this busy, workaday world of ours—would exchange helplessness <note>original: "impotence"</note> for power, weakness and suffering for abounding health and strength, pain and unrest for perfect peace, and poverty of whatever nature for fullness and plenty.

Each person is building their own world. We both build from within and we attract from without. Thought is the force with which we build, for thoughts are forces. Like builds like, and like attracts like. To the degree that thought is spiritualized, it becomes more subtle and powerful in its workings. This spiritualizing process is in accordance with law and is within the power of everyone.

Everything is first worked out in the unseen before it is manifested in the seen, in the ideal before it is realized in the real, and in the spiritual before it shows forth in the material. The realm of the unseen is the realm of cause. The realm of the seen is the realm of effect. The nature of an effect is always determined and conditioned by the nature of its cause.

To point out the great facts in connection with, and the

<vocab>golden thread, religion, prophets, seers, sages, saviors, law, helplessness <note>original: "impotence"</note>, thought, forces, spiritualized, unseen, seen, realm of cause, realm of effect</vocab>

<summary>This preface introduces the "golden thread" of universal truth that links all religions and great figures, arguing that thought is a spiritual force that acts as the primary cause of all material effects.</summary>
<keywords>spirituality, New Thought, creative thought, cause and effect, universal law, mental science</keywords>

--- Page 12 ---
<meta>This page continues the preface, concluding the author's introductory argument about the relationship between the human will and the Divine will.</meta>

great laws underlying the workings of the interior, spiritual thought-forces so simply and clearly that even a child can understand them is the author’s aim. His purpose is to point them out so simply and clearly that everyone can grasp them, and then take them and infuse them into everyday life. By doing so, they may mold life in all its details in accordance with what they wish it to be. To him, the fact that life can be molded in this way is not a matter of mere speculation or theory, but a matter of positive knowledge.

There is a divine sequence running throughout the universe. Within, above, and below the human will, the Divine will works incessantly. To come into harmony with it—and thereby with all the higher laws and forces—and then to align yourself and work in conjunction with them so that they can work in partnership with you, is to enter the chain of this wonderful sequence. This is the secret of all success. This is how one comes into the possession of unknown riches and the realization of undreamed-of powers.

->**R. W. T.**<-

# FULLNESS OF
# PEACE, POWER, AND PLENTY.

## PRELUDE.

The optimist is right. The pessimist is right. The one differs from the other as the light from the dark. Yet both are right. Each is right from his own particular point of view, and this point of view is the determining factor in the life of each. It determines whether it is a life of power or of impotence <note>original: "impotence"; here meaning powerlessness or lack of effectiveness</note>, of peace or of pain, of success or of failure.

The optimist has the power of seeing things in their entirety and in their right relations. The pessimist looks from a limited and a one-sided point of view. The one has his understanding illuminated <note>original: "illumined"</note> by wisdom, while the understanding of the other is darkened by ignorance. Each is building his world from within, and the result of the building is determined by the point of view of each. The optimist, by his superior wisdom and insight, is making his own heaven, and in the degree that he makes his own heaven is he help-

<vocab>optimist, pessimist, wisdom, ignorance, heaven</vocab>

<summary>This introductory text explores how an individual's perspective—whether optimistic or pessimistic—dictates their experience of success, failure, and inner peace. It argues that our internal outlook literally builds the world we inhabit.</summary>
<keywords>optimism, pessimism, perspective, wisdom, internal world-building, power vs. impotence</keywords>

--- Page 16 ---
<meta>This page continues the "Prelude" from page 9, completing the sentence regarding how the optimist and pessimist influence the world through their internal states.</meta>

->Fullness of Peace, Power, and Plenty<-

ing to make one for the rest of the world. The pessimist, by virtue of his limitations, is creating his own hell, and to the extent that he creates his own hell, he is helping to create one for all of humanity.

You and I possess the dominant characteristics of either an optimist or a pessimist. We are therefore creating, hour by hour, our own heaven or our own hell; and to the degree that we are creating one or the other for ourselves, we are helping to create it for everyone else in the world.

The word "heaven" means harmony. The word "hell" comes from the Old English *hell* <note>original: "hell"; the author is likely referring to the root *helan*, meaning to conceal, cover, or hide, though here they interpret it as "to wall in"</note>, meaning to build a wall around or to separate; to be "*helled*" was to be shut off from something. Now, if there is such a thing as harmony, there must be something that one can be in a proper relationship with; for to be in a proper relationship with anything is to be in harmony with it. Again, if there is such a thing as being "*helled*"—shut off or separated—there must be something from which one is held, shut off, or separated.

<summary>This page defines heaven as harmony and hell as separation, arguing that our optimistic or pessimistic outlooks create these states not just for ourselves, but for all of society.</summary>
<keywords>heaven, hell, harmony, separation, etymology, optimism, pessimism, social influence</keywords>

->Fullness of Peace, Power, and Plenty<-

# THE SUPREME FACT OF THE UNIVERSE.

The great central fact of the universe is the Spirit of Infinite Life and Power that lies behind everything, gives life to everything, and expresses itself in and through everything. It is that self-existing principle of life from which everything originated—and not only from which everything has come, but from which everything is continually emerging. If individual life exists, there must necessarily be an infinite source of life from which it comes. If the quality or force of love exists, there must necessarily be an infinite source of love from which it flows. If there is wisdom, there must be an all-wise source behind it from which it springs. The same is true for peace, for power, and for everything we call material things.

There is, then, this Spirit of Infinite Life and Power behind everything, which is the source of all things. This Infinite Power creates, works, and rules through great immutable <note>original: "immutable"; unchanging over time or unable to be changed</note> laws and forces that operate throughout the universe and surround us on every side. Every act of

<summary>This page introduces the "Spirit of Infinite Life and Power" as the fundamental source of all life, love, wisdom, and material existence, operating through unchanging universal laws.</summary>
<keywords>Infinite Power, Source of Life, universal laws, Spirit, metaphysics, immutable laws, creation</keywords>

--- Page 18 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion of the "Spirit of Infinite Life and Power," explaining how universal laws govern both nature and human life, and defining the relationship between the individual soul and the Divine.</meta>

->Fullness of Peace, Power, and Plenty<-

our everyday lives are governed by these same great laws and forces. Every flower that blooms by the wayside springs up, grows, blooms, and fades according to certain great, immutable <note>original: "immutable"; unchanging or unable to be changed</note> laws. Every snowflake that plays between earth and heaven forms, falls, and melts according to certain great, unchangeable laws.

In a sense, there is nothing in all the great universe but law. If this is true, there must of necessity be a force behind it all that is the maker of these laws, and a force greater than the laws that are made. This Spirit of Infinite Life and Power that is behind everything is what I call God. I do not care what term you may use—be it Kindly Light <note>a reference to the famous hymn "Lead, Kindly Light" by John Henry Newman</note>, Providence, the Oversoul <note>original: "Over Soul"; a term popularized by Ralph Waldo Emerson describing a divine spirit that pervades the universe and encompasses all human souls</note>, Omnipotence <note>all-powerful</note>, or whatever term may be most convenient. I do not care what the term may be, as long as we are agreed regarding the great central fact itself.

God, then, is this Infinite Spirit which fills all the universe with Himself alone, so that everything is from Him and in Him, and there is nothing that exists outside of Him. Indeed and in truth, then, in Him we live and move and have our being <note>a reference to the biblical passage Acts 17:28</note>. He is the life of our life, our very life itself. We have received, and we are continually receiving, our life from Him. We are partakers of the life of God; and though we differ from Him in that we are individualized spirits, while He is the Infinite...

<summary>The author argues that all of nature and human existence is governed by immutable laws, which points to a singular, divine source of power—God—who is the very life within every individual.</summary>
<keywords>universal law, God, Oversoul, nature, divine life, spiritual unity, Ralph Waldo Emerson, Providence</keywords>

--- Page 19 ---
<meta>This page continues the philosophical exploration of the relationship between the individual soul and the "Spirit of Infinite Life and Power," arguing that while the human spirit is distinct in degree, it is identical in quality to the divine source.</meta>

->Fullness of Peace, Power, and Plenty<-

Spirit including us as well as everything else besides, yet in essence the life of God and the life of man are identical, and so they are one. They do not differ in essence or quality; they differ only in degree.

There have been and still are highly enlightened <note>original: "illumined"; refers to those with spiritual insight or awakening</note> souls who believe that we receive our life from God like a divine inflow. And again, there have been and still are those who believe that our life is one with the life of God, and therefore that God and man are one. Which is right? Both are right; both are right when they are properly understood.

Regarding the first point: if God is the Infinite Spirit of Life behind everything, from which everything comes, then clearly our lives as individual spirits are continually coming from this Infinite Source by means of this divine inflow. In the second place, if our lives as individual spirits come directly from, and are parts of, this Infinite Spirit of Life, then the amount of the Infinite Spirit that is shown in each life must be identical in quality to that Source. It is just as a drop of water taken from the ocean is identical in nature and characteristics to that ocean, which is its source. 

And how could it be any other way? However, the risk of misunderstanding in this second case is this: although the life of God and the life of man are identical in essence, the life of God so far surpasses <note>original: "transcends"</note> the life...

<summary>The author explains that the human spirit and the Divine are identical in essence and quality, though they differ in degree, using the metaphor of a drop of water compared to the ocean.</summary>
<keywords>divine inflow, Infinite Spirit of Life, individual spirits, essence vs degree, spiritual enlightenment, nature of God</keywords>

--- Page 20 ---
<meta>This page continues the argument that while the life of God and the life of man are different in scale, they are identical in quality. It introduces the metaphor of a mountain reservoir to explain this relationship.</meta>

### Fullness of Peace, Power, and Plenty

...of individual man that it includes everything else besides. In other words, as far as the quality of life is concerned, they are essentially the same; as far as the degree of life is concerned, they are vastly different.

In this light, is it not evident then that both perspectives are true? And even more, that they are one and the same? Both perspectives can be represented by the same illustration.

There is a reservoir in a valley that receives its supply from an inexhaustible reservoir on a mountainside. It is true that the reservoir in the valley receives its supply through the inflow of water from the larger reservoir on the mountainside. It is also true that the water in this smaller reservoir is identical in nature, quality, and characteristics to the water in the larger reservoir that is its source.

The difference, however, is this: the reservoir on the mountainside, in its total volume of water, so far surpasses <note>original: "transcends"</note> the reservoir in the valley that it can supply a countless <note>original: "innumerable"</note> number of similar reservoirs and still remain unexhausted.

And it is the same with human life. If—as I believe we have already agreed, regardless of our other differences—there is an Infinite Spirit of Life behind everything, which is the life of all and the source from which everything comes, then the life of an individual...

<summary>The author uses the metaphor of a mountain reservoir supplying a valley reservoir to illustrate how human life is identical in quality to the Divine source, yet smaller in scale. This comparison resolves the apparent conflict between seeing God as an external "inflow" and seeing God as the internal essence of man.</summary>
<keywords>Infinite Spirit of Life, divine inflow, reservoir metaphor, quality vs degree, individual man, spiritual essence</keywords>

--- Page 21 ---
<meta>This page concludes the argument that human life is an extension of the Divine Source, suggesting that human potential is limited only by self-ignorance.</meta>

# Fullness of Peace, Power, and Plenty

->Page 15<-

...your life and mine must come by a divine inflow from this Infinite Source. And if this is true, then the life that comes by this inflow to man is necessarily the same in essence <note>original: "essence"; the fundamental nature or quality of a thing</note> as this Infinite Spirit of Life. There is a difference, but it is not a difference in essence. It is a difference in degree <note>original: "degree"; the relative extent, amount, or intensity of something</note>.

If this is true, does it not then follow that to the extent that man opens himself to this divine inflow, he approaches God? If so, it then necessarily follows that to the degree that he makes this approach, he takes on the God-powers. And if the God-powers are without limit, does it not then follow that the only limitations man has are the limitations he sets for himself, by virtue of not knowing himself?

# Fullness of Peace, Power, and Plenty

## THE SUPREME FACT OF HUMAN LIFE.

From the great central fact of the universe that we have agreed upon—namely, this Spirit of Infinite Life <note>original: "Spirit of Infinite Life"; the divine or universal source of all existence</note> that is behind everything and from which everything originates—we are led to ask: what is the great central fact of human life? Based on what we have already discussed, the question almost answers itself.

*The great central fact in human life—in your life and in mine—is achieving a conscious, vital realization of our oneness with this Infinite Life, and opening ourselves fully to this divine inflow.* This is the most important fact in human life because it includes everything else; everything else follows naturally from it. To the exact extent that we achieve a conscious realization of our unity with the Infinite Life and open ourselves to this divine inflow <note>original: "divine inflow"; the spiritual energy or influence flowing from the source into the individual</note>, we manifest <note>original: "actualize"; to make a reality of something potential</note> the qualities and powers of that Infinite Life within ourselves.

And what does this mean? It simply means this: we are recognizing our true identity. We are bringing our lives into harmony <note>original: "harmony"; a state of agreement or alignment</note> with the same great laws and forces, and by doing so, we are opening...

<vocab>Spirit of Infinite Life, oneness, divine inflow, human life, harmony</vocab>

<summary>The author identifies the most important fact of human existence as the conscious realization of our connection to the Infinite Life and the opening of ourselves to its power. By aligning with these universal laws, individuals begin to manifest divine qualities in their own lives.</summary>

<keywords>oneness, divine inflow, Infinite Life, human identity, spiritual harmony, self-realization, universal laws</keywords>

--- Page 23 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion on aligning the individual self with the "Infinite Life," explaining how this connection empowers the individual to overcome limitations.</meta>

<page-num>17</page-num>

# Fullness of Peace, Power, and Plenty

...ourselves to the same great inspirations, just as all the prophets, seers, sages, and saviors in the world’s history have done—all those who were truly great and possessed mighty power. For to the extent that we achieve this realization and connect ourselves with this Infinite Source <note>original: "Infinite Source"; the universal, divine origin of all things</note>, we make it possible for higher powers to flow, work, and manifest through us.

We can remain closed to this divine inflow <note>original: "divine inflow"; the spiritual energy or influence flowing from the source into the individual</note>, as well as to these higher forces and powers, through ignorance—as most of us do—and thus hinder or even prevent them from manifesting through us. Or, we can intentionally close ourselves off from their influence and thus deprive ourselves of the powers that are our rightful inheritance by the very nature of our being. On the other hand, we can reach such a vital realization of the oneness of our real selves with this Infinite Life, and can open ourselves so fully to the arrival of this divine inflow and the operation of these higher forces, inspirations, and powers, that we can indeed and in truth become what we might call **God-men** <note>original: "God-men"; individuals who fully embody and express divine qualities in human form</note>.

And what is a God-man? It is one in whom the powers of God are manifesting, even though they remain a human being. No one can set limitations for a man or a woman of this type; for the only limitations he or she can have are those set by the self.

<vocab>Infinite Source, divine inflow, God-men, God-man, Infinite Life, realization, oneness</vocab>

<summary>The author explains that humans can either block or embrace the "divine inflow" of the Infinite Source; by choosing to realize their oneness with this power, they become "God-men" who are limited only by their own beliefs.</summary>
<keywords>divinity, self-realization, Infinite Source, God-man, empowerment, spiritual growth, New Thought</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the exploration of how human potential is unlocked by recognizing one's connection to the divine, moving from the theory of the "God-man" to a practical analogy of a lotus pond.</meta>

<page-num>18</page-num>

# Fullness of Peace, Power, and Plenty

Ignorance is the most powerful factor in setting limits for the majority of humanity; because of this, most people continue to live small, stunted lives simply because they do not realize the larger life to which they are heirs. They have not yet reached a knowledge of the real identity of their true selves.

Humanity has not yet realized that the real self is one with the life of God. Through ignorance, people have not yet opened themselves to the divine inflow <note>original: "divine inflow"; the spiritual energy or influence flowing from the universal source into the individual</note>, and so have never made themselves a channel through which infinite powers and forces can manifest. When we know ourselves merely as human beings, we live accordingly and have only human powers. When we come to realize the fact that we are **God-men** <note>original: "God-men"; individuals who fully embody and express divine qualities in human form</note>, then we live accordingly and possess the powers of God-men. *The more we open ourselves to this divine inflow, the more we are changed from mere humans into God-men.*

A friend has a beautiful lotus pond. A natural basin on his estate—his farm, as he always calls it—is supplied with water from a reservoir in the foothills some distance away. A gate regulates the flow of the water from the main pipe that carries it from the reservoir to the pond.

The difference between the spot as it is now and the lotus pond when our friend gave it his careful attention was caused, as we can easily see, by the shutting of the gate to the pond. This prevented the water from the reservoir in the hills—which was the source of its life—from entering it. ### Fullness of Peace, Power, and Plenty

Before we proceed further, let us briefly consider the nature of thought. Thought is not, as is often supposed, a mere vague abstraction or something of that nature. It is, on the contrary, a vital, living force—the most vital, subtle, and irresistible force in the universe.

In our actual laboratory experiments, we are demonstrating the great fact that thoughts are forces. They have form, quality, substance, and power, and we are beginning to find that there is what we may call a *science of thought*. We are also beginning to realize that through the <term>instrumentality</term> <note>original: "instrumentality"; the means or agency through which something is achieved</note> of our thought-forces, we possess creative power—not just in a metaphorical sense, but actual creative power.

Everything in the material universe around us, everything the universe has ever known, originated first in thought. From thought, it took its form. Every castle, every statue, every painting, every piece of machinery—everything was born and originated first in the mind of the creator before it received its material expression or embodiment. The very universe in which we live is the result of the thought-energies of God, the Infinite Spirit that <term>is back of all</term> <note>original: "is back of all"; meaning the fundamental source or underlying cause of everything</note>. # FULLNESS OF LIFE — BODILY HEALTH AND VIGOR.

God is the Spirit of Infinite Life. If we share in this life and have the power to open ourselves fully to its divine inflow, it means more for our physical lives than we might first realize. Because the life of this Infinite Spirit, by its very nature, cannot contain disease, it follows that no disease can exist in a body where this Spirit enters and flows freely.

Let us recognize from the start that, regarding physical life, *all life moves from the inside out*. There is an unchangeable law <note>original: "immutable law"</note> that says: "As within, so without; cause and effect." In other words, our thought forces, our various mental states, and our emotions all eventually have an effect on our physical bodies.

Someone might say: "I hear a lot these days about the mind’s effect on the body, but I am not sure if I have much confidence in that idea." Don’t you? Imagine someone brings you sudden, shocking news. You grow pale, you <note>The text breaks off here as the author prepares to give examples of physical reactions to mental stimuli.</note>

# *Fullness of Peace, Power, and Plenty*

...is scientifically demonstrating today—that various mental states, emotions, and passions have distinct effects on the body. Each of these, if indulged in to a great extent, induces its own specific forms of disease, and these in time become chronic.

Let us consider for a moment how these operate. If a person is overcome for a moment by, for example, a fit of anger, it triggers a reaction in the physical body <note>original: "organism"</note> that we might rightly call a bodily <term>thunderstorm</term>. This has the effect of souring—or rather, corroding—the body's normal, healthy, and life-giving fluids <note>original: "secretions"</note>. Instead of performing their natural functions, these fluids become poisonous and destructive. If this continues to any great degree, the cumulative effects lead to specific diseases that eventually become chronic.

Conversely, the opposite emotions—kindness, love, benevolence, and goodwill—tend to stimulate a healthy, purifying, and life-giving flow of all the bodily fluids. When these emotions are present, all the channels of the body seem free and open, and life forces flow vigorously <note>original: "bounding"</note> through them. These very forces, once set into such vigorous activity, will in time counteract the poisonous and disease-causing effects of their opposites.

This mental state, shared by the physician, in turn affects the patient's body. Thus, through the influence <note>original: "instrumentality"</note> of this mental suggestion, the healing process continues.

<header>Fullness of Peace, Power, and Plenty</header>

the sooner we begin to look within ourselves for the cause of whatever happens to us, the better it will be. This is because the sooner we do so, the sooner we will begin to create internal conditions that allow only *good* to enter our lives.

By our very nature, we should be masters of all circumstances; yet, because of our ignorance, we are instead mastered by almost countless conditions of every description.

For example, do I fear a draft <note>original: "draft," referring to a current of cool air, which was historically believed to be a direct cause of illness</note>? There is nothing in the draft—which is simply a small, purifying current of God’s pure air—to cause me trouble, to bring on a cold, or perhaps an illness. The draft can affect me only to the degree that *I myself* make it possible, and only to the extent that I allow it to affect me. We must distinguish between actual causes and mere occasions <note>original: "occasions," used here in the philosophical sense of an event that provides an opportunity for a cause to act, but is not the cause itself</note>. The draft is not the cause of sickness, nor does it carry the cause within itself.

Imagine two people sitting in the same draft. One is negatively affected by it, while the other experiences not even a slight inconvenience; in fact, he actually enjoys it. The first person is a victim of circumstances; he fears the draft, recoils from it, and continually thinks about the harm it is doing to him. In other words, he opens every possible avenue for it to enter and take hold of him. Therefore, the draft—which is harmless and beneficial <note>original: "beneficent"</note> in itself—brings him exactly the harm he has empowered it to bring.

But if the draft were the actual cause of illness, it would produce the same results in both people. The fact that it does not show that it is not a cause, but rather a condition; it brings to each person effects that correspond to the conditions it finds within them.

Poor draft! How many thousands—no, millions—of times is it made the scapegoat by those who are too ignorant or too dishonest to look their own weaknesses squarely in the face? Instead of becoming sovereign masters, they remain cringing slaves. Consider what this means! A human being created in the image of the eternal God, a sharer of His life and power, born to have dominion, yet fearing, shaking, and cringing before a little current of pure, life-giving air. But scapegoats are convenient things, even if the only thing they do for us is to assist us in our constant efforts at self-delusion.

The best way to neutralize the bad effects a draft <note>original: "draft," a current of cool air</note> usually has on you is to first create a pure and healthy internal state, and then change your mental attitude toward it. Realize the fact that, on its own, the draft has no power; it only has the power you give it. In this way, you will bring yourself into harmony with it and will no longer live in fear of it. Then, sit in a draft a few times to toughen yourself up to it, which anyone can easily do by approaching it sensibly.

"But what if someone is in poor health, or particularly sensitive to drafts?" In that case, simply start cautiously at first. Do not seek out the strongest draft you can find, especially if you do not yet feel mentally ready for it; if you do not feel ready, it signifies that you are still afraid of it. Good common sense, the ultimate regulator of all life, must be used here just as it is used anywhere else.

If we are born to have dominion <note>original: "dominion," meaning sovereignty or control</note>—and the fact that some people have achieved it proves that we are, because what one person *has* done, everyone can eventually do—then it is not necessary for us to live under the control of any physical force. To the degree that we recognize our own inner powers, we are rulers who are able to give orders; to the degree that we fail to recognize them, we are slaves who are ordered around. We build what—

The whole of human life is cause and effect; there is no such thing as chance in it, nor is there even in all the wide universe. Are we dissatisfied with what comes into our lives? The thing to do, then, is not to spend time railing <note>original: "railing," meaning to complain or protest bitterly</note> against an imaginary something we create and call "fate," but to look within and change the causes at work there, so that things of a different nature may come. For there will come exactly what we cause to come. This is true not only of the physical body, but of all phases and conditions of life. We invite whatever comes, and if we did not invite it—either consciously or unconsciously—it could not and would not come. This may undoubtedly be hard for some to believe, or even to see at first. But to the degree that one candidly and open-mindedly looks at it, and then studies the silent but subtle and—so to speak—all-powerful <note>original: "omnipotent"</note> workings of the thought-forces, and as they trace their effects within them and about them, it becomes clearly evident and easy to understand.

By realizing his oneness with the Infinite Power, and by living continually in this realization.

<page-num>74</page-num>

## Fullness of Peace, Power, and Plenty

The amount of time it takes for the human body to reach maturity, when compared to the growth and lifespan of various animals, suggests that our natural lifespan should be closer to a hundred and twenty years, rather than the shorter lives we commonly see today. Yet, consider the many people around us whose bodies are aging, weakening, and breaking down. They are forced to abandon their bodies long before completing what should be a lengthy period of strong, vigorous middle age.

Because the natural length of life is shortened in this way, it has become what we might call a collective belief <note>original: "race belief"; a common idea or cultural assumption shared by humanity</note> that this shorter span is the natural one. Consequently, when many people approach a certain age and see others starting to show signs of decline—or "going downhill," as we say—they assume it is inevitable for them as well. By adopting this mindset, they often bring these physical conditions upon themselves much earlier than necessary. 

The mind exerts a subtle yet powerful influence on the building and repairing of the body. As we come to understand these forces better, it may become common for people to look forward with joy to the "teens" of their second century <note>referring to the ages of 113 through 119</note>.

A certain friend comes to mind at this moment,

A lady nearly eighty years old. Some—most people, in fact—would call her an old lady, especially those who measure age by the number of seasons that have passed since birth. But to call our friend "old" would be as inaccurate as calling black white. She is no older than a woman of twenty-five; indeed, she is younger—I am happy to say, or perhaps sorry to say, depending on your perspective—than *many* women of that age. By looking for the good in all people and in all things, she has found goodness everywhere. The cheerful personality and voice she possesses today, which attract everyone to her and make her so beautifully appealing, have characterized her entire life. This spirit has, in turn, brought light, hope, courage, and strength to thousands of people over the years, and it appears she will continue to do so for many years to come.

No fears, worries, hatreds, or jealousies; no lingering sorrows or griefs; no greedy pursuit of excessive wealth <note>original: "inordinant gain"; immoderate or socially improper profit-seeking</note> have found a place in her mind. Consequently, her mind—being free from these unhealthy states and conditions—has not manifested <note>original: "externalized"; the process of internal thoughts showing up as physical symptoms in the body</note> in her body the various physical ailments that the great majority of people carry around with them.

And would you like your body to have all the flexibility <note>original: "elasticity"</note>, all the strength, and all the beauty of your younger years? ## THE SECRET, POWER, AND EFFECTS OF LOVE.

This is the Spirit of Infinite Love. The moment we recognize ourselves as one with it, we become so filled with love that we see only the good in everyone. Love grows and reigns supreme. Because of this, we look only for the good, and we find it. This perspective is always rewarding <note>original: "It always pays."</note>.

Since these emotions have a direct effect upon your mind—and through your mind upon your body—they act as a life force added to your own from the outside. You are then continually building this into both your mental and physical life, and so your life is enriched by its influence.

Hatred and all its related emotions are unnatural, abnormal, and perversions; therefore, they are out of harmony with the eternal order of the universe. For if love is the fulfillment of the law, then these opposites are direct violations of that law. There can never be a violation of law without its accompanying pain and suffering in one form or another. There is no escape from this. 

And what is the result of this particular form of violation? When you allow thoughts of anger, hatred, malice, jealousy, envy, criticism, or scorn to take control <note>original: "exercise sway"</note>, they have a corroding and poisoning effect upon the body <note>original: "organism"</note>. They drag it down, and if allowed to continue, they will eventually tear it to pieces by manifesting <note>original: "externalizing"</note> as the specific forms of disease they give rise to. Furthermore, in addition to the destructive influences from your own mind, you are continually attracting the same influences from other minds. These come as destructive forces that increase <note>original: "augmenting"</note> your own, thereby assisting in the process of tearing you down.

I hear it asked: "What about someone who hates me, even though I have never felt that way toward them and am not the cause of them becoming my enemy?" This may be true, but the chances are that you will have very few enemies if there is nothing antagonistic <note>original: "of an antagonistic nature"</note> in your own mind and heart. Make sure there is nothing of this nature. But if hatred should come from another person without any apparent cause on your part, then meet it from beginning to end with thoughts of love and goodwill. In this way, you can—so to speak—neutralize its effects so that it cannot reach you and therefore cannot harm you. Love is

The truly wise man or woman will recognize no one as an enemy. Occasionally we hear the expression, “Never mind; I’ll get even with him.” Will you? And how will you do it? You can do it in one of two ways.

You can, as you have in mind, deal with him as he deals—or apparently deals—with you, and "pay him back in his own coin" <note>original: "pay him... in his own coin" (to treat someone in the same negative way they treated you)</note>. If you do this, you will "get even" with him by sinking yourself to his level, and both of you will suffer by it. 

Or, you can show yourself to be the bigger person. You can send him love in exchange for hatred and kindness in exchange for ill-treatment. In this way, you "get even" with him by raising him to a higher level. But remember that you can never help another person without that very act helping yourself; and if you are selfless <note>original: "forgetful of self"</note>, then in almost all cases the value to you is greater than the service you provide <note>original: "render"</note> to another. 

If you are ready to treat him as he treats you, then you show clearly that there is something in you that draws hatred and ill-treatment toward you. You deserve what you are getting and should not complain; nor would you complain if you were wise. However, all growth in life happens from the inside out. A person becomes a true master only to the degree that the realization of the divinity within his own nature dawns upon his inner consciousness. This realization brings him to a knowledge of higher laws. There is no way we can more effectively speed up this dawning in another person's inner consciousness than by demonstrating the divinity within ourselves simply by the way we live.

We do this by example and not by rules <note>original: "precept"</note>. By living, not by preaching. By doing, not by professing. By living the life ourselves, not by laying down dogmatic rules as to how it should be lived. There is no influence as powerful <note>original: "no contagion equal to the contagion of life"</note> as the energy of a life well-lived. Whatever we sow, we shall also reap, and every seed produces its own kind. We can "kill" others not only by causing direct physical injury, but we also "kill" through every hostile <note>original: "antagonistic"</note> thought.

Not only do we kill in this way, but while we kill others, we commit suicide ourselves. To not love is to not truly live; or rather, it is to live a living death. The life that reaches out in love to everyone is the life that is full, rich, and continually expanding in beauty and power. This is the kind of life that becomes increasingly inclusive, and therefore grows larger in its scope and influence. The greater the man or woman, the more inclusive they are in their love and their friendships. Peter and John <note>Two of the twelve apostles of Jesus, as described in the New Testament</note> were one day going up to the temple, and as they were entering the gate, they were met by a poor cripple who asked them for alms <term>alms: money, food, or other donations given to the poor</term>. He thus brought him into a condition where he could help himself. In other words, the greatest service we can do for another is to help him to help himself. To help him directly might be weakening, though not necessarily. It depends entirely upon circumstances. But to help one to help himself is never weakening; it is always encouraging and strengthening because it leads him to a larger and stronger life.

## WISDOM AND INTERIOR ILLUMINATION.

This is the Spirit of Infinite Wisdom, and to the extent that we open ourselves to it, the highest wisdom manifests itself to and through us. In this way, we can go to the very heart of the universe itself and find the mysteries that are hidden to the majority of mankind—hidden to them, though not hidden by their own nature.

To achieve the highest wisdom and insight, we must have absolute confidence that the Divine is guiding us, but not through the channel of someone else. And why should we go to another for knowledge and wisdom? With God, there is no favoritism <note>original: "no respect of persons," a biblical phrase meaning God does not show partiality or prefer one person over another</note>. Why should we seek these things secondhand? Why should we stifle <note>original: "stultify"</note> our own innate powers in this way? Why should we not go directly to the Infinite Source itself?

Consequently, he always does the right thing at the right time and in the right way. He always knows when and how to act, and he is never in the state of being a house divided against itself.

In this way, we come into a knowledge of truth where we no longer need facts that are continually changing. We can then enter into the quiet of our own inner selves. We can open the window and look out, and thus gather the facts as we choose. This is true wisdom. "Wisdom is the knowledge of God." Wisdom comes through intuition. It far surpasses mere knowledge. A great deal of knowledge—knowing many individual things—can be acquired simply through a very strong memory. This comes through <term>tuition</term> <note>original: "tuition"; here meaning external instruction or formal schooling, contrasted with internal "intuition."</note>. But wisdom far transcends knowledge, because knowledge is merely a minor byproduct of this deeper wisdom.

Anyone who wishes to enter the realm of wisdom must first strip away all intellectual pride. He must become like a little child. Prejudices, preconceived opinions, and fixed beliefs always block the path to true wisdom. Arrogant opinions are always self-destructive in their influence. They bar the door against the entrance of truth.

Everywhere around us, we see people in the religious, scientific, political, and social worlds who, through intellectual pride, are so wrapped up in their own arrogance and prejudices that larger and more recent revelations of truth can find no way into them. When the steam engine was still being developed and before it was perfected enough for practical use, a well-known Englishman—famous in scientific circles at the time—wrote a long pamphlet proving that it would be impossible for it *ever* to be used for ocean travel. He argued that it would be impossible for any ship to carry enough coal to fuel its furnace for a trip across the ocean. The most interesting part of the story is that the very first steamship to cross from England to America carried among its cargo copies of the first edition of that carefully prepared pamphlet. Only one edition was ever printed. Many more might be sold today <note>The author suggests the pamphlet would be popular now as a humorous example of failed skepticism</note>.

This is indeed an amusing fact; but it is even more amusing to see a person who voluntarily closes themselves off from the truth simply because <note>original: "forsooth"; an archaic word meaning "in truth" or "indeed," often used sarcastically to highlight a ridiculous claim</note> it does not arrive through conventional, orthodox, or here—

In Eastern literature <note>This likely refers to a famous parable found in Hindu and Buddhist traditions, famously used by Swami Vivekananda in 1893 to illustrate religious narrow-mindedness.</note>, there is a fable about a frog. This frog lived in a well, and he had never been outside of his little well. One day, a frog whose home was in the sea came to his well. Being interested in all things, the sea frog went inside. "Who are you? Where do you live?" asked the frog in the well. "I am so-and-so, and my home is in the sea." "The sea? What is that? Where is it?" "It is a very large body of water, and it is not far away." "How big is your sea?" "Oh, it is very big." "Is it as big as this?" the well frog asked, pointing to a little stone lying nearby. "Oh, much bigger." "Is it as big as this?" he asked, pointing to the board upon which they were sitting. "Oh, much bigger." "How much bigger, then?" "Why, the sea in which I live is bigger than your entire well; it would contain millions of wells like yours." "Nonsense, nonsense; you are a deceiver and a liar. Get out of my well! Get out of my well! I want nothing to do with any frogs like you!"

"You shall know the truth, and the truth shall make you free," is the promise. But if you close yourselves to the truth and live only in your own prejudices <note>original: "conceits"</note>, those prejudices will make fools and idiots of you. This statement would apply to many people, including many who pride themselves on their superior intellectual achievements. Idiocy is a state of arrested mental growth. Closing oneself off to truth—and therefore to growth—for whatever reason brings about a certain type of idiocy, even if it is not called by that name. 

On the other hand, another type of arrested growth is caused by taking all things for granted without proving them for oneself, simply because they come from a particular person, a particular book, or a particular institution. This is caused by always looking outside oneself <note>original: "without"</note> instead of being true to the light within, and carefully tending it so that it may give an ever-clearer light.

With the brave and fearless <note>original: "intrepid"</note> Walt Whitman, we should all be able to say:

> "From this hour I declare <note>original: "ordain"</note> myself freed <note>original: "loos’d"</note> from limits and imaginary lines,

> Listening to others, considering well what they say,
> Pausing, searching, receiving, contemplating,
> Gently, but with undeniable will, stripping away <note>original: "divesting myself of"</note> the attachments <note>original: "holds"</note> that would hold me back."

If the fault lies within us, then the cure for this unnatural state also lies within us. It is never necessary to fall into such a state if we are alert and remain awake to the light and the powers within us. The light is always shining; our only responsibility is to ensure <note>original: "diligently to see to"</note> that we allow nothing to come between us and that light.

# Fullness of Peace, Power, and Plenty

When the life of realization <note>The internal process of making divine truths a personal reality in one's own experience.</note> is lived, you will find that no good thing will be withheld, for all things are included in this state. If one is willing to trust himself *fully* to the Law <note>The author refers to "Divine Law" or "Universal Law," the spiritual principles believed to govern the universe.</note>, the Law will never fail him. It is half-hearted trust that brings uncertain, and therefore unsatisfactory, results. Nothing is firmer and surer than the Divine. It will never fail the person who throws himself wholly upon it. The secret of life, then, is to live continually in this realization, whatever one may be doing and wherever one may be—by day and by night, both waking and sleeping. This state can be lived in while we are sleeping no less than when we are awake. And here, shall we consider a few facts regarding sleep, and how we may receive instruction and illumination <note>Spiritual enlightenment or sudden insight.</note> while asleep?

It is for this reason that those who are susceptible will "catch a cold"—as we call it—more easily when the body is tired or exhausted through loss of sleep than at almost any other time. The body is in a state where outside influences can affect it more easily than when it is in its normal condition. And when they do have an effect, they always strike the weakest parts first.

As the body grows finer in this way—in other words, as the process of its evolution is accelerated—it in turn helps the mind and the soul in the realization of ever higher perceptions. Sleep, then, is for the resting and the rebuilding of the body. The soul needs no rest, and while the body is at rest in sleep, the soul's life is just as active as when the body is busy.

There are some, having a deep insight into the soul's activities, who say that we travel when we sleep. Some are able to recall and bring over into their conscious, waking life the scenes they visited, the information they gained, and the events that have taken place <note>original: "transpired"</note>. Most people are not able to do this, and so much that might otherwise be gained is lost. They say, however, that it is in our power—to the extent that we understand the spiritual laws—to go where we will, and to bring over into our conscious, waking life all the experiences we have gained. Regardless of whether this is the case, it is certainly true that while sleeping we have the power, in a perfectly normal and natural way, to receive much of value through spiritual light, instruction, and growth that the majority of people now miss.

If the life of the soul, which relates us to the Infinite Spirit <note>The "Infinite Spirit" refers to a universal, divine energy or God-presence, a central concept in the New Thought movement of the late 19th century.</note>, is always active even while the body is at rest, why may the mind not direct our state of being as we fall asleep? By doing so, while the body is at rest, the mind may continually receive enlightenment <note>original: "illumination"</note> from the soul and bring what it receives into our conscious, waking life. This is why the highest and truest insights often come in the early morning hours, before the activities of the day and their accompanying distractions have had an influence. We can use this quiet, receptive, impressionable period most effectively by wisely directing the mind’s activities along the highest and most desirable paths, and thus, so to speak, set the tone <note>original: "set the pace"</note> for the day.

## THE REALIZATION OF PERFECT PEACE.

This is the Spirit of Infinite Peace, and the moment we come into harmony with it, an inflowing tide of peace comes to us, because peace is harmony. A deep interior meaning underlies the great truth: "To be spiritually minded is life and peace." To recognize the fact that we are spirit and to live with this thought is to be spiritually minded, and therefore to be in harmony and peace. 

Oh, the thousands of men and women all around us who are weary with care, troubled, and ill at ease, running here and there <note>original: "hither and thither"</note> to find peace—weary in body, soul, and mind; going to other countries, traveling the world over, returning, and still not finding it. Naturally, they have not found it and they never will find it in this way, because they are looking for it where it does not exist. They are looking for it on the outside <note>original: "without"</note> when they should look within. Peace is to be found only within, and unless a person finds it there, he will never find it at all.

Peace does not lie in the external world. It lies within one’s own soul. We may travel over...

# Fullness of Peace, Power, and Plenty

Within each person lies the cause of whatever happens to them. Each individual has the power to determine what comes into their life. Everything in the visible, material world originates in the unseen, spiritual realm—the <term>thought world</term> <note>The "thought world" refers to the internal realm of a person's mindset, beliefs, and mental focus, which the author argues creates external reality.</note>. This internal realm is the world of cause, while the former (the material world) is the world of effect. The nature of the effect is always in harmony with the nature of its cause. Whatever a person experiences in their invisible thought world, they are continually making real <note>original: "actualizing"</note> in their visible, material world. If they want their conditions to be different in the material world, they must make the necessary change in the mental world. 

A clear realization of this great fact would bring success to thousands of men and women all around us who are currently in the depths of despair. It would bring health—overflowing health and strength—to thousands who are now diseased and suffering. It would bring peace and joy to thousands who are currently unhappy and restless.

## COMING INTO FULLNESS OF POWER.

This is the Spirit of Infinite Power, and to the degree that we open ourselves to it, power becomes manifest in us. With God all things are possible—that is, in partnership <note>original: "conjunction"</note> with God all things are possible. The true secret of power lies in keeping one’s connection with the God who works <note>original: "worketh"</note> all things; and to the extent <note>original: "in the degree"</note> that we keep this connection, we are able to literally rise above every conceivable limitation.

Why, then, waste time running here and there <note>original: "hither and thither"</note> to acquire power? Why waste time with this practice or that practice? Why not go directly to the mountaintop itself, instead of wandering through the byways, in the valleys, and on the mountainsides? The teaching that man has absolute dominion <note>term: "dominion" — supreme authority or the power to govern and control</note>, as found in all the scriptures of the world, is true not of physical man, but of the *spiritual man*. There are many animals, for example, larger and stronger, over which he would not have dominion from a physical standpoint, but he can gain supremacy over even

To the exact degree that a person recognizes themselves as a spiritual being and lives accordingly, they are able to exceed the power of someone who identifies only as a material being. All the world's sacred literature is full of examples of what we call miracles. These are not limited to any specific time or place. There is no "age of miracles" that is distinct from any other period; any era can be an age of miracles. Whatever has occurred in world history can happen again through the workings of the same laws and forces. These miracles were not performed by beings who were more than human, but by those who—through recognizing their unity <note>original: "oneness"</note> with God—became God-humans, allowing higher forces and powers to work through them.

We must ask: what is a miracle? Is it something supernatural? It is supernatural only in the sense that it is above the ordinary "natural" state, or rather, above what is natural for a person in their standard state of being. If you preserve your individuality, then you become a master; and if you are wise and discreet, your influence and power will be an aid in bringing—

<page-num>170</page-num>
# Fullness of Peace, Power, and Plenty

It is the kind of preacher who has not yet understood the vital spirit of Jesus’s teachings who is emptying our churches. As a result, instead of sharing this spirit, he gives the people outdated rituals, dogmas <note>original: "dogmas"; established beliefs or doctrines held to be authoritative</note>, and mere speculations. This type of leader seems to focus his primary efforts on getting people ready to die. The Germans have a saying: "Never do the second thing before the first." We need teachers who will first show us how to live. Living, after all, always comes before dying. It is also true that once we know how to live—and actually live according to that knowledge—then "dying," as we call it, will take care of itself in a wonderfully beautiful way. In fact, that is the only way it can truly be addressed.

Because our churches are emptying, and because people are tiring of receiving nothing but husks <note>original: "husks"; the dry, worthless outer shell of grain, used here to describe superficial religious forms lacking spiritual nourishment</note>, many short-sighted people often say that religion is dying out. Religion dying out? How can anything die before it is even born? As far as the people are concerned, religion is only just being born; or rather, they are just now waking up to a vital, everyday religion. We are just beginning to move beyond the literal words <note>original: "the mere letter"; a reference to 2 Corinthians 3:6, "for the letter kills, but the Spirit gives life"</note> and into its real, living spirit. Religion dying out? That is impossible.

# Fullness of Peace, Power, and Plenty

to conceive of. Religion is as much a part of the human soul as the human soul is a part of God. And as long as God and the human soul exist, religion will never die.

Much of the dogma, the form, the ceremony, and the mere literal words <note>original: "the mere letter"; a reference to focusing on the surface-level rules or literal text of scripture rather than its spiritual essence</note> that have stood as religion—and let us be fair enough to say, often held quite honestly—this, thank God, is rapidly dying out, and never so rapidly as it is today. It is dying in two ways. 

First, there is a large class of people tired of, or even nauseated by, it all, who conscientiously prefer to have nothing rather than this. They are simply abandoning it, just as a tree abandons its leaves when the early winter comes. 

Second, there is a large class in whom the Divine Breath <note>original: "Divine Breath"; the vital, life-giving spirit of God within the individual</note> is stirring, who are finding the "Christ within" <note>original: "Christ within"; the idea of an indwelling divine presence or the spark of divinity in every person</note> in all its matchless beauty and redeeming power. And this new life is pushing off the old, just as in the spring the newly awakened life in a tree pushes off the old, lifeless leaves that have clung on during the winter to make place for the new ones. Witnessing the way this "dead-leaf religion" is being pushed off on every hand is indeed most interesting and inspiring.

## PLENTY OF ALL THINGS — THE LAW OF PROSPERITY.

This is the Spirit of Infinite Plenty <term>Spirit of Infinite Plenty: a core concept in New Thought philosophy that views the divine as an inexhaustible source of both spiritual and material wealth</term>, the Power that has brought—and is continually bringing—all things into expression in material form. One who <note>original: "He who"</note> lives in the realization of their <note>original: "his"</note> oneness with this Infinite Power becomes a magnet to attract to themselves a continual supply of whatever things they desire.

If a person holds themselves <note>original: "If one hold himself"</note> in the thought of poverty, they will be poor, and the chances are that they will remain in poverty. If they hold themselves—whatever current conditions may be—continually in the thought of prosperity, they set into motion forces that will sooner or later bring them into prosperous conditions. The law of attraction <term>law of attraction: the belief that "like attracts like," meaning that a person's focused thoughts (positive or negative) bring corresponding experiences into their life</term> works unceasingly throughout the universe, and the one great and never-changing fact in connection with it is, as we have found, that like attracts like. If we are one with this Infinite Power, this source of all things, then in the degree that we live in the realization of this oneness, in that degree do we actualize in ourselves a power that will bring to us an abun- <note>The text likely continues with the word "abundance" on the following page.</note>

I know a young woman who recently needed money very badly. She needed it for a good cause, and she saw no reason why she shouldn’t have it. She was a bit surprised that they asked her to do this specific type of work, but she told herself, "This is a calling. I will respond and see where it leads." She took on the job and did it well. When she finished, she was paid much more than she expected. She felt the payment was too large for the work she had performed, and she protested.

# Fullness of Peace, Power, and Plenty

Many times, those who do not think much about these things themselves are actually the most practical people the world knows. On the other hand, those who take great pride in boasting of their own "practicality" are often the least practical people of all. In certain narrow ways they may be practical, but as far as life in its entirety is concerned, they are absurdly impractical.

What profit is there, for example, for a man who has gained the whole world in material terms, but has never yet truly known his own soul? <note>This is a reference to the biblical verse Matthew 16:26: "For what is a man profited, if he shall gain the whole world, and lose his own soul?"</note> There are crowds of people all around us who are completely missing out on real life; they have not learned even the basics <note>original: "a, b, c"</note> of true living. They are slaves—miserable slaves—to their temporary material possessions <note>original: "accumulations"</note>. These are people who think they own their wealth when, in reality, they are completely owned by it. Their lives are relatively empty of service to those around them and to the world at large. When they can no longer inhabit the body—the tool <note>original: "agency"</note> through which they interact with the material world—they will leave this life poor indeed, even pitiably poor. Unable to take even the smallest bit of their wealth with them, they will enter the next form of life naked and destitute.

# Fullness of Peace, Power, and Plenty

...character, the realized powers of the soul, the real riches of the inner life and spiritual growth <note>original: "unfoldment"</note>, and all those things that become our real and eternal possessions, have been given no place in their lives. We must not imagine that habits, once formed, are any easier to break in the next life than they are in this one. If one voluntarily cultivates a certain obsession <note>original: "mania"</note> here, we must not assume that the mere shedding of the physical body <note>original: "dropping of the body"</note> makes all conditions perfect. Everything is governed by law; everything is cause and effect. As we sow, so shall we also reap—not only in this life, but in all lives.

Anyone who is enslaved by the sole desire for material possessions here will continue to be enslaved even after they can no longer keep their physical body. Furthermore, they will then lack even the means to satisfy those desires. Dominated by this habit, they will be unable—at least for a time—to focus their affections on other things. This desire, without any way to fulfill it, will be doubly agonizing to them. 

Perhaps <note>original: "Perchance"</note> this suffering may be increased by seeing the wealth they thought was theirs now being scattered and wasted by reckless spenders <note>original: "spendthrifts"</note>. They leave their property to others through a will, as we say, but they can have no say in how it is used.

# Fullness of Peace, Power, and Plenty

How foolish, then, for us to think that any material possessions *are truly ours*. How absurd it is, for example, for someone to fence off a number of acres of God’s earth and claim they are *theirs*. Nothing is ours that we cannot keep forever. The things that come into our hands do not come for the purpose of being owned <note>original: "possessed"</note>, as we say, much less for the purpose of being hoarded. They come into our hands to be used—to be used wisely. We are merely stewards, and as stewards, we shall be held accountable for the way we use whatever is entrusted to us. That great law of compensation <note>The principle that every action has an equal reaction, or that what we give is what we eventually receive</note> that runs through all of life is wonderfully exact in its workings, although we may not always fully understand it, or even recognize it when it operates in our own lives.

The person who has realized the higher life <note>A state of spiritual awareness or enlightenment</note> no longer has a desire to accumulate enormous wealth, any more than they have a desire for any other kind of excess. To the degree that a person recognizes that they are wealthy within, external wealth becomes less important in their estimation. When someone realizes that there is a source within from which they can draw a power to attract <note>original: "call to him"</note> and manifest <note>original: "actualize"</note> in their hands at any time a sufficient supply for all their needs, they no longer burden themselves with vast material

It is as hard for a rich man to enter into the kingdom of heaven, said the Master <note>original: "the Master"; referring to Jesus</note>—he who, having nothing, had everything—as it is for a camel to pass through the eye of a needle. In other words, if a man gives all his time to the accumulation and the hoarding of outward material possessions far beyond what he can possibly ever use, what time has he for the finding of that wonderful kingdom? When that kingdom is found, it brings all else with it. Which is better: to have millions of dollars and the burden of taking care of it all—for the one always involves the other—or to come into the knowledge of such laws and forces <note>The metaphysical or spiritual principles that the author believes govern the universe and provide for human needs</note> that every need will be supplied in good time? Is it better to know that no good thing shall be withheld, and to know that we have it in our power to make the supply always equal to the demand?

The person who enters into the realm of this higher knowledge never cares to bring upon himself the species of insanity that has such a firm hold upon so many in the world today.

Wealth beyond a certain amount cannot be used, and when it cannot be used, it then becomes a hindrance rather than an aid—a curse rather than a blessing. Everywhere around us are people whose lives are now stunted and dwarfed, who could instead make them rich and beautiful, filled with a constant <note>original: "perennial"</note> joy, if they would begin to wisely use the resources they have spent the greater portion of their lives accumulating.

->#### Fullness of Peace, Power, and Plenty<-

Great numbers of people are constantly preventing higher and better things from reaching them because they are forever clinging to the old. If they would use and pass on the old, room would be made for new things to arrive. Hoarding always brings loss in one form or another. Using—wisely using—brings an ever-renewing gain.

If a tree were to ignorantly and greedily hold on to this year’s leaves after they had served their purpose, what would happen to the full and beautiful new life that should emerge in the spring? Gradual decay and eventually death would be the result. If the tree is already dead, then it might be fine for it to cling to the old, for no new leaves will ever come. But as long as the life within the tree is active, it must shed the old leaves so that room can be made for the new.

Opulence <term>Opulence: A state of great wealth or luxurious abundance; in this context, the author refers to a divine or natural law of plenty that provides for all needs.</term> is the law of the universe—an abundant supply for every need, provided nothing is placed in the way of its arrival. The natural and normal life for us is this: to have such a fullness of life and power by living so constantly in the realization of our oneness with the Infinite Life and Power that we find ourselves in constant possession of an abundant supply of everything we need.

# Fullness of Peace, Power, and Plenty

When he <note>Jesus</note> said, "Man shall not live by bread alone, but by every word that proceeds out of the mouth of God" <note>Matthew 4:4</note>, he expressed a truth of far greater importance than we have yet begun to fully grasp. Here he taught that even physical life cannot be maintained by material food alone, but that one’s connection with this Infinite Source determines, to a very great extent, the condition of even the bodily structure and activities. "Blessed are the pure in heart, for they shall see God" <note>Matthew 5:8</note>. In other words, blessed are those who recognize only God in all the universe, for by such people, God shall be seen.

The great Hindu sage Manu <note>A legendary lawgiver and figure in Hindu tradition</note> said, "He who perceives the Supreme Soul in all beings within his own soul, and acquires equanimity <note>evenness of mind or composure</note> toward them all, attains the highest bliss." It was Athanasius <note>A 4th-century Christian theologian and Church Father</note> who said, "Even we may become gods walking about in the flesh." The same great truth we are considering is the one that runs through the life and the teachings of Gautama—he who became the Buddha. He said that people are in bondage because they have not yet removed the idea of the "*I*" <note>the ego or sense of a separate self</note>. To do away with all sense of separateness and to recognize the unity <note>original: "oneness"</note> of the self with the Infinite is the spirit that breathes through all his teachings. This same great truth—union with God—also ran through the lives of all the medieval mystics.

Then, coming nearer to our own time, we find the highly enlightened visionary Emanuel Swedenborg <note>a Swedish theologian and scientist (1688–1772) known for his spiritual writings</note> pointing out the great laws regarding what he called the "divine influx" <note>original: "divine influx"; Swedenborg's term for the continuous flow of life, love, and wisdom from God into the human soul</note>, and how we may open ourselves more fully to its workings. The great central fact in the religion and worship of the Friends <note>commonly known as the Quakers</note> is the "inner light"—God in the soul of man speaking directly to the exact degree that the soul is opened to Him. The inspired visionary who lived among us in Concord <note>referring to Ralph Waldo Emerson (1803–1882), a leader of the Transcendentalist movement</note> recognized the same great truth when he said, "We are all inlets to the great sea of life." And it was by opening himself so fully to its inflow that he became a person of such inspiration.

Throughout world history, we find that the men and women who have entered into the realm of true wisdom and power—and therefore into the realm of true peace and joy—have lived in harmony with this Higher Power. David <note>the biblical King David</note> was strong and powerful, and his soul burst forth in praise and adoration to the exact degree that he listened to the voice of God and lived in accordance with his higher spiritual guidance. Whenever he failed to do this, we hear his soul crying out in anguish and lamentation. The same is true of every nation or people. When the Israelites acknowledged God and followed His guidance, they were prosperous and contented,

All the prophets, seers, sages, and saviors in world history became who they were, and consequently possessed the powers they had, through an entirely natural process. They all recognized and reached a conscious realization of their oneness with the Infinite Life. God does not show favoritism <note>original: "God is no respecter of persons"; a biblical phrase meaning God does not prefer one individual over another based on status or rank</note>. He has no "chosen people"; instead, here and there a race or nation chooses to respect God and consequently lives the life of a "chosen people."

->*Fullness of Peace, Power, and Plenty*<-

There has never been a specific age or location for miracles that is separate from any other time or place. What we call miracles have occurred in abundance in all places and at all times whenever the conditions for them have been met. They are being performed today just as much as they ever have been, provided that the laws governing them are respected. We are told they were "mighty men"—mighty men who "walked with God" <note>This biblical phrase implies living in constant awareness of and alignment with the Divine</note>. In the phrase "walked with God" lies the secret of why they were called "mighty men." It is a simple matter of cause and effect.

The Lord never grants prosperity to a man arbitrarily; rather, a man prospers because he acknowledges the Lord and lives in accordance with higher laws. Solomon was given the opportunity to choose whatever he desired; his better judgment prevailed, and he chose wisdom <note>Referring to the story in 1 Kings 3, where King Solomon asks God for an "understanding heart" to lead his people rather than asking for wealth or long life</note>. But when he chose wisdom, he found that it included everything else besides. 

We are told in traditional texts that God hardened Pharaoh’s heart <note>A reference to the Book of Exodus, where Pharaoh refuses to let the Israelites leave Egypt</note>. I do not believe that. God never hardens anyone's heart. Pharaoh hardened his own heart, and God was blamed for it. But when Pharaoh hardened his heart and disobeyed the voice of God, the plagues came. Again, this was a matter of cause and effect. Had he, on the contrary, listened—in other words, had he opened himself to and obeyed the voice of God—the plagues would not have come.

# Fullness of Peace, Power, and Plenty

We can be our own best friends, or we can be our own worst enemies. To the degree that we become friends to the highest and best within ourselves, we become friends to everyone; and to the degree that we become enemies to the highest and best within ourselves, we become enemies to everyone. 

To the degree that we open ourselves to higher powers and let them manifest through us, we become—through the very inspirations we carry—saviors <note>original: "saviours"; here meaning those who help or preserve others from harm or spiritual decay</note> of our fellow human beings in a sense. In this way, we all are, or can become, each other's saviors. In this way, you may indeed become one of the world’s redeemers <note>original: "redeemers"; those who restore or liberate others, often in a spiritual sense</note>.

### THE BASIC PRINCIPLE OF ALL RELIGIONS — THE UNIVERSAL RELIGION.

The great truth we are considering is the fundamental principle running through all religions. We find it in every one. In this regard, they all agree. It is, moreover, a great truth about which all people can agree, whether they belong to the same or to different religions. People always quarrel about trifles <note>original: "trifles"; meaning things of little value or importance</note>—about their personal views on minor, insignificant points. They always come together in the presence of great fundamental truths, the common threads of which run through everything. Quarrels are connected to the lower self <note>lower self: the ego-driven, worldly, or selfish part of a person's nature</note>; agreements are connected to the higher self <note>higher self: the spiritual, enlightened, or divine part of a person's nature</note>.

A community may have factions that quarrel and fight among themselves, but if a great calamity strikes the land—such as a flood, famine, or pestilence <note>original: "pestilence"; a fatal epidemic disease</note>—these small personal differences are entirely forgotten. Everyone works shoulder to shoulder in one great cause. The changing, evolving self gives rise to quarrels, but the permanent, <unclear>real</unclear> self unites everyone in the highest efforts of <unclear>love</unclear> and service.

<term>Universal Religion</term>: The concept that a single, underlying spiritual truth exists within all different faith traditions.
<term>fundamental principle</term>: A basic truth or law that serves as the foundation for a system of belief.
<term>lower self</term>: The aspect of human nature focused on ego, division, and material concerns.
<term>higher self</term>: The spiritual or divine aspect of human nature that seeks unity and love.
<term>evolving self</term>: The part of the personality that is constantly changing and growing, often prone to conflict.
<term>real self</term>: The eternal and unchanging core of a person’s being.

<summary>This page explores the idea of a "Universal Religion" based on shared fundamental truths that unite all people, contrasting the divisive "lower self" with the unifying "higher self."</summary>

<keywords>universal religion, higher self, lower self, spiritual unity, fundamental truths, human cooperation, New Thought</keywords>

->Fullness of Peace, Power, and Plenty<-

Patriotism is a beautiful thing; it is well for me to love my country, but why should I love my own country more than I love all others? If I love my own and hate others, I then show my limitations, and my patriotism will not even stand the test for my own nation. If I love my own country and, in the same way, love all other countries, then I show the greatness of my nature. A patriotism of this kind is noble and can always be relied upon.

The view of God on which we are agreed—that He is the *Infinite Spirit of Life and Power* that is behind everything, working in and through all, and is the life of all—is a matter on which all people and all religions can agree. With this view, there can be no infidels <note>original: "infidels"; historically, those who do not believe in a particular religion or have no religious faith</note> or atheists. There are certainly atheists and infidels in connection with many specific views that are held concerning God, and thank God there are. Even devout and earnest people among us attribute things to God that no respectable men or women would permit to be attributed to themselves. This view is satisfying to those who cannot see how God can be angry with his children, or be jealous and vindictive <note>original: "vindictive"; having a strong or unreasoning desire for revenge</note>. A display of these qualities always lessens our respect for men and women, and yet we still attribute them to God.

Christ is preeminently <note>original: "preëminently"</note> a type of the universal. John the Baptist is a type of the personal. John dressed in a particular way, ate a particular kind of food, belonged to a particular order, and lived and taught in a particular locality; he himself recognized the fact that he must decrease while Christ must increase. Christ, on the other hand, gave himself absolutely no limitations. He allowed himself to be bound by nothing.
